> DEVELOPER

What's the elegant but effective approach?

> AGENT

Use a single **distance-to-target policy** that drives messaging and queue ordering, but never scoring.

1. Add one policy function (single source of truth):
- `mode = execution` if gap `>= 10` (or stagnating)
- `mode = balanced` if gap `4..9.9`
- `mode = analysis_ready` if gap `<= 3.9`
- Add small hysteresis (1 point) to avoid mode flip-flop.

2. Put mode into narrative/query payload each run:
- `strict_target_gap`
- `progress_mode`
- `mode_reason`

3. Use mode in comms:
- Scan/status always print north star line + mode.
- In `execution`: message is fix loop only (`next -> fix/resolve -> rescan`), not analysis-first wording.

4. Use mode in `next` ranking:
- In `execution`, softly demote `review`/subjective items behind mechanical work.
- Do not hard-block: if no mechanical items exist, review items surface normally.

5. Keep integrity guardrails:
- Do **not** pass target/mode into subjective assessment scoring.
- Only guide workflow order and comms, never reviewer scoring behavior.

6. Add explicit override:
- `--focus review` (or config flag) to bypass bias when user intentionally wants analysis now.

That’s elegant because it’s one policy reused everywhere, and effective because it changes behavior where it matters (`scan` guidance + `next` selection) without corrupting scoring integrity.

> DEVELOPER

i mean elegant from an agent workflow perspective

> AGENT

The elegant workflow is a **mode-gated loop** with hard guardrails, not a smart-but-loose planner.

1. Compute `gap = target_strict - strict`.
2. Set `mode`:
- `execution` if `gap >= 10`
- `balanced` if `4 <= gap < 10`
- `analysis_ready` if `gap < 4`

3. Enforce allowed actions by mode:
- `execution`: only fix/resolve/rescan commands from top mechanical actions.
- `balanced`: mostly fix loop, allow short targeted analysis if blocked.
- `analysis_ready`: allow deeper review/design analysis.

4. Use one tight loop:
- `scan -> pick 1 action -> implement -> resolve -> rescan`
- Repeat until mode changes.

5. Add anti-drift rule:
- If mode is `execution`, block “direct analysis” suggestions unless:
- no mechanical open items, or
- strict score has not moved for 3 scans.

6. Keep agent output minimal and consistent each cycle:
- `North star`: strict X -> target Y (gap Z)
- `Mode`
- `Chosen action`
- `Result delta`
- `Next command`

That gives you elegance because the agent behavior is deterministic and explainable, and effectiveness because it keeps momentum toward target instead of getting stuck in analysis early.

> DEVELOPER

I don't think we should enforce it because the user may want something else. I think we should like heavily nudge it based on for example if they're under the score their primary goal should be to get to the score.

> AGENT

Agreed. The best design is **soft pressure, never hard blocking**.

Use a “score pressure” signal and apply it everywhere as a nudge:

1. Compute pressure from gap:
- `gap = max(0, target_strict - strict_score)`
- `pressure = clamp(gap / 12, 0, 1)`

2. Re-rank actions (don’t remove any):
- Boost: `auto_fix`, `mechanical T1/T2`, high-impact refactors.
- Demote (while pressure is high): `review`, `holistic/design analysis`, low-impact cleanup.
- If no strict movement for 3 scans, reduce the demotion so analysis can surface.

3. Make scan comms pressure-aware:
- Always show `North star: current -> target (gap)`.
- High pressure wording: “Primary goal is closing strict gap; stay in fix->resolve->rescan loop.”
- Low pressure wording: “You can invest in deeper review/design now.”

4. Keep user intent supreme:
- If user asks for analysis/review directly, do it immediately.
- Add optional hint flag like `--focus review` / `--focus execution` to override nudges.

5. Keep one-cycle cadence:
- `scan -> one prioritized action -> resolve -> rescan`
- This keeps momentum visible and avoids planning sprawl.

This gives you a strong behavioral bias toward target progress, without enforcing a rigid workflow.

```json
{
  "cmd": "cat > /tmp/issue_score_pressure_nudges.md <<'MD'\n## Summary\n\nIntroduce a **score-pressure workflow layer** that keeps users and agents continuously oriented toward the strict-score target across `scan`, `next`, `plan`, and related commands.\n\nCore intent:\n- The user should always be aware of the target score and current gap.\n- When the gap is large, guidance should strongly bias toward **execution loops** (`fix -> resolve -> rescan`) over direct analysis detours.\n- This should be a **nudge**, not enforcement: users can always choose a different path.\n\n## Why this is needed\n\nCurrent behavior exposes target context in some places (for example `next` prints a “North star” line), but prioritization can still drift to design-analysis/review work even when strict score is far below target.\n\nObserved in practice:\n- With strict significantly below target, `next` can surface a review/design item first.\n- `scan` accepts `target_strict_score` context but does not consistently convert that into score-first guidance.\n- There is no single, shared “distance-to-target” policy reused by queue ordering + narrative messaging.\n\nResult: users/agents can lose momentum on the primary objective (closing strict-score gap) and enter analysis-heavy loops too early.\n\n## Product goal\n\nMake “working toward the strict target score” the default operating mode in every command surface, while preserving user autonomy.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- Do **not** hard-block analysis/review commands.\n- Do **not** hide any work items.\n- Do **not** alter scoring formulas.\n- Do **not** pass target-anchoring instructions into subjective scoring/reviewer prompts.\n\n## Approaches considered\n\n### 1) Hard enforcement (reject analysis while far below target)\n\nExample: disallow review/deep-analysis actions until strict reaches threshold.\n\nPros:\n- Maximum focus on target.\n\nCons:\n- Violates user intent flexibility.\n- Can block valid workflows (e.g., when analysis is required to unblock execution).\n\nDecision: **reject**.\n\n### 2) Messaging-only nudges\n\nExample: always print target gap and suggest fix loop, but do not influence ranking/order.\n\nPros:\n- Very safe and simple.\n\nCons:\n- Not strong enough; queue may still surface non-progress work first.\n\nDecision: insufficient alone.\n\n### 3) Soft-pressure policy across ranking + messaging (recommended)\n\nExample: compute a pressure score from target gap and use it to:\n- strengthen score-first wording,\n- re-rank (not remove) execution items above analysis items when pressure is high,\n- relax pressure when close to target or stagnation indicates analysis is needed.\n\nPros:\n- Strong behavior bias with no hard lock.\n- Works across all commands with one shared model.\n- Preserves user control.\n\nDecision: **recommended**.\n\n## Recommended design\n\n### A) Canonical score-pressure computation\n\nAdd one reusable helper (single source of truth) based on strict target gap:\n\n- `gap = max(0, target_strict - current_strict)`\n- `pressure = clamp(gap / 12.0, 0.0, 1.0)`\n\nAnd a mode label (for messaging):\n- `execution` when gap is large\n- `balanced` when mid-gap\n- `analysis_ready` when near target\n\nOptional hysteresis (recommended):\n- Add a 1-point deadband so mode doesn’t oscillate scan-to-scan.\n\n### B) Command behavior under pressure (nudge only)\n\n#### `scan`\n\n- Always print north-star progress (`current -> target`, gap).\n- Include mode-specific guidance:\n  - high pressure: “primary goal is closing strict gap; stay in fix/resolve/rescan loop.”\n  - low pressure: “analysis/refinement is acceptable now.”\n- Keep existing warnings/integrity messaging.\n\n#### `next`\n\n- Re-rank items using pressure-weighted preference:\n  - boost mechanical execution items (auto-fix, T1/T2, high-impact refactor)\n  - softly demote review/design-analysis items while pressure is high\n- Never remove items; only reorder.\n- If no mechanical path exists (or no strict movement for N scans), ease demotion.\n\n#### `plan`\n\n- Start plan with execution lane when pressure is high.\n- Keep analysis lane visible as optional/secondary.\n\n#### `status`\n\n- Always show target progress + mode.\n- Show “primary priority this cycle” derived from pressure-aware narrative.\n\n### C) User intent overrides\n\nAdd explicit opt-in overrides (names TBD):\n- `--focus review`\n- `--focus execution`\n- `--no-score-pressure`\n\nIf user explicitly asks for analysis/review, immediately honor it.\n\n### D) Integrity guardrails\n\n- Do not include pressure/target anchoring in subjective review scoring packets.\n- Preserve anti-gaming safeguards already in place.\n- Maintain transparency: print why an item is prioritized (e.g., pressure-aware ranking rationale in explain mode).\n\n## Implementation map (candidate touchpoints)\n\n### Narrative / policy core\n\n- `desloppify/intelligence/narrative/core.py`\n  - compute strict target context (already exists)\n  - add `progress_pressure`, `progress_mode`, `mode_reason`\n- `desloppify/intelligence/narrative/types.py`\n  - type additions for new fields\n- `desloppify/intelligence/narrative/strategy_engine.py`\n  - allow strategy hint to consume pressure/mode\n- `desloppify/intelligence/narrative/headline.py`\n  - phrase headline with score-priority emphasis under high pressure\n\n### Scan comms\n\n- `desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan.py`\n- `desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_analysis.py`\n- `desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_summary.py`\n- `desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_llm.py`\n\n### Work queue ordering\n\n- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py`\n  - pass pressure context into queue build\n- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py`\n  - pressure-aware sort key adjustments\n- `desloppify/app/commands/next.py`\n  - wire target/gap/pressure into queue options\n\n### Status/plan renderers\n\n- `desloppify/app/commands/status_cmd.py`\n- `desloppify/app/commands/status_parts/render.py`\n- `desloppify/app/commands/plan*` (where plan ranking text is produced)\n\n### Config / flags\n\n- optionally extend config for defaults/tuning:\n  - `score_pressure_enabled` (default true)\n  - pressure threshold tuning if needed\n\n## Behavior details (proposed)\n\n### Pressure-aware ranking heuristic (example)\n\nTake existing sort priority and add a soft penalty term:\n\n- `analysis_penalty = pressure * analysis_weight`\n- where `analysis_weight` applies to:\n  - review findings\n  - subjective synthetic dimension tasks\n  - low-impact exploratory refactors\n\nKeep constraints:\n- no filtering,\n- stable deterministic ordering,\n- explicit user focus flag overrides penalty.\n\n### Stagnation escape hatch\n\nIf strict score has not improved for 3+ scans, automatically reduce analysis penalty even under high pressure to avoid local minima.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n1. Every `scan`, `next`, `plan`, and `status` run shows strict target progress context.\n2. When strict is far below target, default suggestions/ranking prioritize execution loop work.\n3. No command is blocked; review/analysis remains available on explicit request.\n4. Existing subjective-review anti-anchoring integrity is preserved.\n5. Unit tests cover pressure computation, ordering effects, and override behavior.\n6. Regression tests confirm no hiding/removal of work items.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- `tests/core/test_narrative.py`\n  - pressure/mode computation and narrative fields.\n- `tests/scan/test_scan_reporting_direct.py`\n  - pressure-aware scan messaging.\n- `tests/review/test_work_queue.py`\n  - ranking changes under high/low pressure, plus no-item-loss checks.\n- `tests/commands/test_cmd_next.py`\n  - high-gap vs low-gap top item behavior; override flags.\n- `tests/commands/test_cmd_status.py` / plan tests\n  - consistent target awareness text.\n\n## Rollout suggestion\n\n1. Phase 1: add pressure fields + scan/status messaging.\n2. Phase 2: apply pressure-aware re-ranking in `next` and `plan`.\n3. Phase 3: tune thresholds based on real usage feedback.\n\n## Open questions\n\n1. Should thresholds be fixed or config-tunable initially?\n2. Should pressure use only strict gap, or include trend (delta over last N scans) from day one?\n3. Should `show` command also print mode summary, or only interactive priority commands (`scan/next/plan/status`)?\nMD"
```

As of **February 26, 2026**, I reviewed all open issues in `peteromallet/desloppify`.

Only one clear unresolved bug worth immediate action:

1. **#149** https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify/issues/149  
   - Real bug for users on PyPI today.  
   - PyPI latest is still **0.7.2** (uploaded **2026-02-23**), and its metadata does **not** include the `full` extra, so `desloppify[full]` warns.
   - `main` now has `full` in `pyproject.toml`, so this looks **fixed in code but not released**.

The other open issues (**#140, #147, #152, #153**) are feature/workflow enhancements, not broken behavior bugs.

Brief recommendation:
1. Ship a new PyPI release from current `main` immediately.  
2. Verify with a clean install: `pip install --upgrade "desloppify[full]"` (no warning).  
3. Close #149 once verified.  
4. Optionally add a small release-check step in CI so package metadata and docs can’t drift again.

> TOOL

tool_use apply_patch
```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/tests/ci/test_ci_contracts.py\n@@\n from __future__ import annotations\n \n import re\n+import tomllib\n from pathlib import Path\n \n import pytest\n@@\n MAKEFILE = REPO_ROOT / \"Makefile\"\n+README = REPO_ROOT / \"README.md\"\n+PYPROJECT = REPO_ROOT / \"pyproject.toml\"\n@@\n def _step_names(job: dict) -> list[str]:\n     return [step.get(\"name\", \"\") for step in job.get(\"steps\", [])]\n+\n+\n+def _optional_dependencies() -> dict[str, list[str]]:\n+    doc = tomllib.loads(PYPROJECT.read_text())\n+    optional = doc.get(\"project\", {}).get(\"optional-dependencies\", {})\n+    return {str(key): list(value) for key, value in optional.items()}\n@@\n def test_makefile_contains_ci_gate_targets() -> None:\n@@\n     assert expected.issubset(targets)\n+\n+\n+def test_readme_optional_extras_exist_in_pyproject() -> None:\n+    readme = README.read_text()\n+    referenced = set(re.findall(r\"desloppify\\[([a-zA-Z0-9_-]+)\\]\", readme))\n+    optional = _optional_dependencies()\n+    missing = sorted(extra for extra in referenced if extra not in optional)\n+    assert not missing, (\n+        \"README references optional extras that are not defined in pyproject.toml: \"\n+        f\"{missing}\"\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def test_full_extra_includes_all_optional_dependency_groups() -> None:\n+    optional = _optional_dependencies()\n+    full = set(optional.get(\"full\", []))\n+    missing_dependencies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}\n+    for extra, deps in optional.items():\n+        if extra == \"full\":\n+            continue\n+        extra_missing = sorted(dep for dep in deps if dep not in full)\n+        if extra_missing:\n+            missing_dependencies[extra] = extra_missing\n+\n+    assert not missing_dependencies, (\n+        \"Optional extras must stay represented in [full] so README install guidance \"\n+        f\"does not drift: {missing_dependencies}\"\n+    )\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

```json
  "cmd": "set -euo pipefail\nfor i in {1..20}; do\n  echo \"attempt=$i\"\n  curl -fsS https://pypi.org/pypi/desloppify/json -o /tmp/desloppify-pypi.json || true\n  if [ -s /tmp/desloppify-pypi.json ]; then\n    latest=$(jq -r '.info.version' /tmp/desloppify-pypi.json)\n    echo \"latest=$latest\"\n    if [ \"$latest\" = \"0.7.7\" ]; then\n      jq -r '.info.requires_dist[]? | select(test(\"extra ==\"))' /tmp/desloppify-pypi.json\n      break\n    fi\n  fi\n  sleep 3\ndone\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Yes, effectively yes.

`desloppify` applies excludes globally before commands run, and `review --prepare` uses that filtered file list:

- Excludes are merged from CLI + config and set globally in [`cli.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/cli.py#L39).
- Review setup pulls files via `lang.file_finder(...)` in [`runtime.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/runtime.py#L29), which goes through discovery with exclusion filtering in [`file_discovery.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/file_discovery.py#L166).
- Subjective selection also skips non-production zones (`test/generated/vendor`) in [`selection.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/intelligence/review/selection.py#L74).

So excluded paths should not be fed into subjective review packets.

so the subjective review agents know not to review excluded places?

Yes in normal flow.

They’re not told via a special “don’t review excluded paths” instruction; instead, excluded paths are filtered out before packet generation, so those files are not included in what reviewers receive.  
So practically, subjective agents only review the non-excluded, production-scoped files they’re given.

> AGENT

They don’t review the whole repo by default in batch mode.

- Batch reviewers are given an immutable packet and per-batch `files_to_read` lists, and the prompt explicitly says “evaluate ONLY listed files” in [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L117).
- In your latest packet [holistic_packet_20260226_222712.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_20260226_222712.json), the scope is **111 unique files** across 5 batches.

Important detail for your current setup:
- That packet was generated with `config.exclude = []` (inside the packet), so it includes both `source/...` and `Wan2GP/...`.
- Your current config now has `exclude: ["Wan2GP/**"]`, but you need to regenerate the review packet for that to take effect in reviewer scope.

If you want, I can regenerate it now and confirm `Wan2GP/` is fully absent from `files_to_read`.

> DEVELOPER

is it not regenerated at agent deploy time? what about sub agents who don't get their own package?

> AGENT

I’ll verify this from the review runner flow so I can answer exactly: when packets are regenerated, and how subagents get scope without their own package install.

> AGENT

Correct: it is **not** regenerated at agent deploy (`update-skill`) time.

- Deploy/update skill just writes instruction docs, no review packet build: [update_skill.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/update_skill.py#L130).
- Packet regeneration happens when you run review flows:
  - `review --run-batches` (default path) builds a fresh packet each run: [batch.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch.py#L112), called from [batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py#L371).
  - `review --external-start` also builds a fresh packet: [external.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py#L130).
- Exception: if you pass `--packet`, it reuses an old immutable snapshot instead of regenerating: [parser_groups_admin.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py#L365).

For subagents without their own package:
- They don’t need `desloppify` installed. Orchestrator sends them prompt + packet and runs them in repo workspace: [runner_helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py#L141).
- Scope is prompt-driven (“ONLY listed files/dimensions”): [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L117).

So if exclude config changed, rerun `review --run-batches` **without** `--packet` (or run `review --prepare`/`--external-start`) to refresh scope.

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
  "cmd": "gh issue create -R peteromallet/desloppify --title \"Gate subjective reruns behind a drained plan + auto-clear stale subjective plan items\" --body-file - <<'EOF'\n## Summary\nSubjective review reruns should be **end-of-loop work**, not something we do while active plan items are still open.\n\nRequested workflow policy:\n- Before rerunning subjective review, the active plan should be fully cleared.\n- If stale subjective review entries are still hanging around, clear them out first so they don’t block or pollute the next cycle.\n\n## Why this matters\nRight now it’s possible to rerun subjective review while the plan still has unresolved work. That causes churn:\n- open queue keeps changing while we’re still executing old commitments,\n- stale subjective markers can keep reappearing and steal attention,\n- teams get pulled into review cycles too early instead of finishing the current plan.\n\nThis hurts momentum and makes the plan feel less trustworthy as the source of truth.\n\n## Desired behavior\n### 1) Plan-drained precondition for subjective rerun\nWhen running:\n- `desloppify review --prepare`\n- `desloppify review --run-batches ...`\n- `desloppify review --external-start ...`\n\nif there are still open actionable plan items, block rerun by default and print:\n- open item count,\n- top remaining items,\n- exact commands to finish or resolve them.\n\n### 2) Clear stale subjective entries before rerun\nBefore starting a new subjective cycle:\n- detect stale subjective review plan items/findings from previous cycle,\n- mark/archive/resolve them in a deterministic way,\n- report exactly what was cleared and why.\n\nGoal: new subjective run starts from a clean queue state, not a mixed stale+fresh state.\n\n### 3) Keep an explicit escape hatch\nProvide override for advanced users/emergencies, e.g.:\n- `--force-review-rerun`\n\nDefault should remain strict: no rerun until plan is clear.\n\n## Scope proposal\n- Add a shared preflight guard used by all subjective review entry paths.\n- Add stale-subjective cleanup routine invoked in that preflight.\n- Integrate with plan/queue rendering so users can see when they are blocked and what to do next.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n1. Subjective rerun commands are blocked when plan has open actionable items.\n2. CLI output explains blocker counts and next commands clearly.\n3. Stale subjective plan entries are auto-cleared (or clearly archived) before rerun.\n4. A forced override exists and is explicit in output when used.\n5. Tests cover:\n   - blocked rerun with open plan,\n   - allowed rerun with empty plan,\n   - stale cleanup behavior,\n   - override path.\n\n## Related\n- #156 (stale subjective marker re-open behavior)\n- #153 (active planning mode / durable plan workflow)\nEOF"
}
```

I’ve got the Python LOC count; I’m quickly running one more pass across all code languages (still excluding tests) so you get the complete number too.

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
  "cmd": "set -euo pipefail\nREPO=/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226\ncd \"$REPO\"\nif command -v cloc >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"METHOD=cloc\"\n  # Count Python code lines, excluding any directory named tests and typical test files.\n  cloc --json --quiet --include-lang=Python \\\n    --not-match-d='(^|/)tests($|/)' \\\n    --not-match-f='(^|/)(test_.*\\.py|.*_test\\.py)$' \\\n    . > /tmp/desloppify_cloc.json\n  python - <<'PY'\nimport json\nj=json.load(open('/tmp/desloppify_cloc.json'))\npy=j.get('Python',{})\nprint('code_lines', int(py.get('code',0)))\nprint('comment_lines', int(py.get('comment',0)))\nprint('blank_lines', int(py.get('blank',0)))\nprint('total_physical_lines', int(py.get('nFiles',0)) and int(py.get('code',0))+int(py.get('comment',0))+int(py.get('blank',0)))\nprint('files_counted', int(py.get('nFiles',0)))\nPY\nelse\n  echo \"METHOD=rg+wc\"\n  files=$(rg --files -g '*.py' -g '!**/tests/**' -g '!**/test_*.py' -g '!**/*_test.py' .)\n  file_count=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$files\" | sed '/^$/d' | wc -l | tr -d ' ')\n  total_lines=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$files\" | sed '/^$/d' | xargs wc -l | tail -n1 | awk '{print $1}')\n  code_lines=$(printf '%s\\n' \"$files\" | sed '/^$/d' | xargs awk 'BEGIN{c=0} {if ($0 ~ /^[[:space:]]*$/) next; if ($0 ~ /^[[:space:]]*#/) next; c++} END{print c}')\n  echo \"code_lines $code_lines\"\n  echo \"total_physical_lines $total_lines\"\n  echo \"files_counted $file_count\"\nfi"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

is it beautiful?

> AGENT

Not yet, but it’s getting there.  
Engineering quality is strong in places (tests, CI contracts, release hygiene), but “beautiful” needs less workflow churn and tighter plan/review coherence.

> DEVELOPER

What does that mean?

> AGENT

It means:

- It’s **functional and increasingly reliable**.
- It’s **not yet consistently elegant** end-to-end.

Specifically:
- Good: strong safety rails, solid test coverage, clear command surface.
- Not beautiful yet: too much workflow/state complexity (plan vs review vs stale markers), and some user paths still feel heavier than they should.

“Beautiful” here would mean: same power, but fewer edge-case loops, clearer defaults, and smoother day-to-day flow.

> DEVELOPER

Can you share some examples of edge case loops? Do we have actually detectors for that as part of this? Can you go through each of those things you mentioned and share examples?

> AGENT

I’ll break this down concretely with code-level examples: the loop patterns, what currently catches them (detector vs guardrail), and what’s missing. I’m pulling the exact implementation points now.

> AGENT

Yes. Concrete examples below, with whether they’re detector-backed.

1. **Holistic stale re-open loop**: you can resolve `subjective_review::.::holistic_stale`, then a later scan reopens it after age/file-drift checks. This is implemented in [review_coverage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/engine/detectors/review_coverage.py#L206) and wired into scan in [shared_phases.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/shared_phases.py#L369).  
Has detector: **Yes** (`subjective_review` in [registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/core/registry.py#L314)).

2. **Old packet scope loop**: if `--packet` is used, review can reuse stale scope (including files now excluded), instead of regenerating from current config. Packet override path is in [batch.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch.py#L112) and flag is in [parser_groups_admin.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py#L365).  
Has detector: **No** (this is workflow state, not code detector).

3. **Plan snapshot loop**: `plan` is regenerated from current state each run; there is no durable editable plan state, so manual ordering/context can be re-lost after rescans. See [plan_cmd.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/plan_cmd.py#L23) and [render.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/engine/planning/render.py#L291).  
Has detector: **No** (product/workflow gap, tracked by issue #153).

4. **Review-first priority loop**: open review findings are always ranked first in `next`, which can pull you into analysis/review before execution cleanup. Policy is explicit in [ranking.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py#L85).  
Has detector: **No** (queue policy behavior, tracked by issue #152).

5. **Partial-dimension rerun loop**: you can run subset dimensions, then get warned to rerun missing dimensions, causing repeated cycles. Warnings are in [batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py#L214) and [batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py#L260).  
Has detector: **No** (guardrail warning only).

6. **Import validation loop**: one invalid finding can fail closed and force rerun/fix payload loops unless `--allow-partial`. See [import_helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/import_helpers.py#L603).  
Has detector: **No** (validation gate, intentional strictness).

What detectors you *do* have for staleness-like debt:
1. `subjective_review` (unreviewed/changed/stale + holistic stale): [review_coverage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/engine/detectors/review_coverage.py#L47).  
2. `stale_exclude`: [scan_helpers.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_helpers.py#L16), registered in [registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/core/registry.py#L292).  
3. `stale_wontfix`: [scan_wontfix.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_wontfix.py#L141), registered in [registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/core/registry.py#L270).

And yes, subagents are *intended* to stay scoped to packet files (`ONLY listed files`) in [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L117), but that’s prompt policy rather than a hard filesystem sandbox.

> AGENT

Checked. Short answer: **mostly no**.

I inspected the last 6 merged subjective-review runs (`20260226_222712`, `222053`, `214930`, `213624`, `192229`, `191935`) and compared findings against the workflow loops I flagged (packet reuse, plan churn, review-first ranking, partial-dimension rerun loop, fail-closed import loop, exclude-scope loop).

What I found:
- The subjective-review findings are architectural/codebase issues (e.g. boundary leaks, ownership fragmentation), not tool-workflow loop issues.
- Across those 6 runs, there were **0 direct matches** for those workflow-loop patterns in review findings.
- One exception from scanner-side markers: `subjective_review::.::holistic_stale` is present in state and is currently reopened a lot (`reopen_count=10`), plus many `::changed` subjective markers are reopening.

So:
- **Subjective reviewers are not currently surfacing those tool/workflow loops as findings.**
- The only one being surfaced today is via the built-in `subjective_review` coverage/staleness detector logic (not from reviewer-authored findings).

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
  "cmd": "cd /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP && python - <<'PY'\nimport json, pathlib\nst= json.loads(pathlib.Path('.desloppify/state-python.json').read_text())\nfindings=st.get('findings',{})\n# summarize subjective_review findings\nsubs=[f for f in findings.values() if f.get('detector')=='subjective_review']\nopen_sub=[f for f in subs if f.get('status')=='open']\nprint('subjective_total',len(subs),'subjective_open',len(open_sub))\n# exact loop markers\nfor marker in ['subjective_review::.::holistic_stale','subjective_review::.::holistic_unreviewed']:\n    f=findings.get(marker)\n    if not f:\n        print(marker,'ABSENT')\n        continue\n    print(marker,'status=',f.get('status'),'reopen_count=',f.get('reopen_count'),'summary=',f.get('summary'))\n# changed markers churn\nchanged=[f for f in subs if isinstance(f.get('id',''),str) and f.get('id','').endswith('::changed')]\nopen_changed=[f for f in changed if f.get('status')=='open']\nprint('changed_total',len(changed),'changed_open',len(open_changed),'changed_reopened>=2',sum(1 for f in open_changed if int(f.get('reopen_count',0) or 0)>=2))\n# imported subjective findings queue (review issues)\nissues=st.get('review_issues',{})\nopen_issues=[i for i in issues.values() if i.get('status')=='open']\nprint('review_issues_total',len(issues),'review_issues_open',len(open_issues))\n# check if any review_issue mentions loop terms\nterms=['holistic_stale','packet','plan','ranking','allow-partial','coverage gap','missing dimensions']\nhits=[]\nfor i in open_issues:\n    text=' '.join([str(i.get('summary','')),str(i.get('suggestion','')),' '.join(i.get('evidence',[]) if isinstance(i.get('evidence'),list) else [])]).lower()\n    if any(t in text for t in terms):\n        hits.append((i.get('id'),i.get('summary')))\nprint('review_issue_loop_term_hits',len(hits))\nfor hid,s in hits[:10]:\n    print(' -',hid,':',s)\nPY"
}
```

```json
}
```

> AGENT

Yes. After checking the latest runs, the main reason is scope mismatch plus prompt design.

- Latest subjective runs are narrowly scoped to code-quality dimensions, not workflow diagnostics.  
  Example: [holistic_findings_merged.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_222712/holistic_findings_merged.json) has findings only in `cross_module_architecture`, `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance`.
- The run itself confirms partial scope (`full_sweep_included: false`, only 3 imported dimensions): [run_summary.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_222712/run_summary.json).
- Subagent prompt explicitly tells reviewers to evaluate only assigned files/dimensions, so they are not asked to audit tool workflow loops: [batch_prompt_template.py#L117](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L117).
- The loops I flagged are mostly state/workflow phenomena (`holistic_stale`, reopen churn, packet reuse, rerun gating), not pure code defects in target files. Those are generated by scanner/state logic, not subjective reviewer findings.
- You can see that in state: [state-python.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/.desloppify/state-python.json) has `subjective_review::.::holistic_stale` reopened many times and many `::changed` reopeners, while subjective review findings themselves don’t describe those loops.
- So missing them is mostly expected under current design, not reviewer failure.

If you want these caught reliably, they need first-class detector/guardrail checks (not just subjective review).

> DEVELOPER

Why is it silently filtered out? Is that like a design mistake? Is there a poke??

> AGENT

I traced the exact path; I’ll give you a direct answer with the specific spots where the concerns batch is appended, then dropped, and why there’s no warning today.

> AGENT

Yes. It’s getting dropped by dimension scoping, and currently that drop is silent.

- The concerns batch is always created as `dimensions: ["design_coherence"]` in [prepare_batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py#L512).
- Your active review packet was scoped to only `cross_module_architecture`, `high_level_elegance`, `mid_level_elegance` in [review_packet_blind.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json#L5).
- Batch filtering keeps only batches whose dimensions overlap selected dims and drops others in [prepare_batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py#L427).
- The concerns batch is appended, then filtered, with no warning path in [prepare.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify-release-fix-20260226/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py#L267).

So: not a logic bug in filtering, but it is a UX/design gap (observability bug).  
And no, there isn’t a real “poke” today for this specific case.

Best poke to add: if a generated concerns batch is dropped by dimension filter, emit a reminder/warning like:
- “Mechanical concern signals were skipped because `design_coherence` is not in active dimensions.”
- Suggested command: rerun with `--dimensions ...,design_coherence`.

**What I changed**

1. Prevent silent concern-drop in scoped runs  
- Passed active dimensions into concern batch creation in [prepare.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py:272).  
- Updated concern batching logic in [prepare_batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py:559):
  - Keeps default concern dims (`design_coherence`, `initialization_coupling`) when present.
  - If neither is selected, maps concern batch to active dimensions (`mapped_to_active_dimensions=true`) so it can’t be filtered out.
  - Adds explicit metadata: `concern_signals`, `concern_signal_count`, `mapped_to_active_dimensions`.

2. Feed mechanical concerns directly into subagent prompts  
- Added rendered concern hypothesis block in [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py:100).  
- Added requirement to explicitly confirm/refute concern signals in [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py:144).

3. Added workflow-loop review questions (the “meta” issues)

> DEVELOPER

Did you add any questions or prompts to surface the issues that you had flagged earlier or the things that you missed or would they be captured by the new things that you found?

> AGENT

Yes, I added explicit prompt guidance for those missed/flagged patterns.

New prompt additions now ask reviewers to check for:

- stale/reopen churn loops
- generated-but-dropped packet/batch paths
- ranking/triage starvation of target-improving work
- rerun-before-drain behavior (re-running while prior review work is still open)

These were added in the batch prompt template and are now present in generated subagent prompts.  
Plus, mechanical concern signals are now injected as explicit hypotheses (confirm/refute), so those issues are more likely to surface even when dimensions are scoped.

So: both are true now:
- direct new questions for the specific misses
- stronger concern-signal feed that should also catch related cases.

> DEVELOPER

And is the prompts that we add are they correlated with the kind of mechanical issues that we unearth? So for example if we discover a specific mechanical issue then we feed that to the prompt. Do we adjust our prompt based on that?

> AGENT

I’ll verify exact line refs for the dynamic prompt wiring so I can answer precisely.

> AGENT

Yes, now they’re correlated.

- Mechanical findings are converted into detector-aware concern questions in [concerns.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/concerns.py#L239).
- Those are injected into review batches as `concern_signals` in [prepare_batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches.py#L559).
- We pass active dimensions into that step in [prepare.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py#L272), and if scoped dims would have dropped concern dims, we remap instead of dropping (`mapped_to_active_dimensions`).
- Prompt text then includes those signals and tells reviewers to confirm/refute them in [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L100) and [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py#L252).

So yes, prompt content now adapts based on mechanical issues found.  
What we still don’t do is fully rewrite the entire base system prompt per detector; we inject dynamic sections into a stable template.

> DEVELOPER

How could we make the prompts template beautiful?

> AGENT

Best path is to turn it from one giant string into a small prompt-composer pipeline.

1. Split into composable sections  
Move logic from [batch_prompt_template.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch_prompt_template.py) into section renderers:
- `header`
- `seed_files`
- `historical_focus`
- `mechanical_concern_signals`
- `dimension_focus`
- `workflow_integrity`
- `task_contract`
- `output_schema`

2. Use a typed `PromptContext`  
Define one dataclass with all inputs (`dims`, `files`, `concern_signals`, `historical_issue_focus`, etc.) so each section reads from one stable object.

3. Make section inclusion declarative  
Use a registry like:
```python
SectionSpec(id="workflow_integrity", when=lambda ctx: has_arch_dims(ctx), render=...)
```
No more scattered `if` logic.

4. Make dimension guidance data-driven  
Move per-dimension text to a mapping table/module instead of hardcoded string blocks.  
That makes adding/changing dimensions clean and diff-friendly.

5. Auto-number requirements  
Generate `1, 1a, 1b...` from a requirements list so numbering never drifts when adding/removing items.

6. Add snapshot tests for prompt shape  
Golden tests for:
- normal batch
- concern batch
- scoped-dim mapped concern batch
- historical+concern+workflow combo

7. Add a prompt budget check  
Optional: estimate token size and trim low-priority sections predictably when large.

If you want, I can do this refactor next in a clean, incremental PR-style patch.

You’re right. I missed them because I only ran targeted tests before pushing, not the full `make tests-full` gate. That let cross-suite regressions slip.

I fixed and pushed a follow-up commit to `peteromallet/desloppify`:

- Commit: `bd2100d`
- Fixes:
  - restored `opencode` mapping in `core/skill_docs.py`
  - removed remaining runtime `from desloppify.utils import ...` imports in Dart/GDScript phases
  - adjusted load-error flow so direct `raise_load_errors()` raises (as tested) while `load_all()` logs without aborting

Re-runs of the previously failing tests now pass locally:
- `test_from_install_path_match_opencode`
- `test_runtime_code_avoids_deprecated_compat_facades`
- `test_raise_load_errors_includes_module_name_and_exception_type`
- `test_load_all_surfaces_import_failures`

I’ll keep full-suite pre-push as the default gate from here.

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
  "cmd": "gh issue comment 153 --repo peteromallet/desloppify --body \"+1 more thing to make this really work in practice: we should cluster items, not just reorder them.\n\nSpecifically, cluster both when we build the plan and when we push/update plan items from scan/next/review.\n\nWhy: flat item lists still create churn/whiplash even with a living doc. Clusters preserve strategy context and make progress legible across finding ID churn.\n\nSuggested clustering axes:\n- root cause / epic (boundary leak, ownership confusion, duplicated orchestration, etc.)\n- architectural seam or package area\n- dependency chain (what must land first)\n\nIf an underlying finding is superseded, keep the cluster and remap members instead of dropping context. That keeps planning memory intact while details evolve.\""
}
```

> AGENT

You’re right; here’s why it slipped and what’s fixed.

Why tests didn’t catch it earlier:
1. I only ran targeted subsets pre-push, not full `make tests-full`.
2. The suite was aborting on an unhandled `KeyboardInterrupt` path in the review batch runner, so later failures were masked.
3. That path is only exercised by the specific keyboard-interrupt test case, which I hadn’t included in the first targeted set.

I fixed and pushed:
1. Keyboard interrupt handling in review batch execution so it exits as `SystemExit(130)` with partial summary/log (instead of killing pytest):
[desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py)
2. Restored `PROJECT_ROOT` compatibility hook expected by tests in per-file import:
[desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/per_file.py)
3. Restored per-file review tier behavior expected by current scoring tests:
[desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/shared.py)

Validation:
- `make tests-full PYTEST_XML=pytest-full.xml` now passes locally:
- `3727 passed, 124 skipped`

Pushed commit:
- `7fdcb2c` to `peteromallet/desloppify` `main`

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
```
```json
  "cmd": "gh issue create --repo peteromallet/desloppify --title \"Auto-refresh and report updated score/scorecard after all score-changing operations\" --body-file - <<'EOF'\n## Summary\nWe should automatically recompute and report the latest score (and scorecard) whenever a workflow step materially changes scoring state.\n\nRight now this is too easy to leave stale. You do the work (import reviews, resolve findings, mark wontfix, etc.) but don’t immediately see the new truth unless you manually re-scan or run another command.\n\n## Why this matters\n- Score trust: stale score output makes it hard to trust progress.\n- Workflow momentum: users should see immediate feedback after score-moving actions.\n- Better prioritization: the very next decision (plan/next/fix) should use current score, not old score.\n\n## Proposed behavior\nAfter any operation that can change objective/strict/subjective scoring, automatically:\n1. Refresh scoring state (incremental where possible, full recompute fallback).\n2. Print a concise delta summary (`before -> after`) for key scores.\n3. Render/update scorecard output in the same command response.\n4. Persist updated score artifacts (`query.json`, scorecard snapshot, state fields) so follow-up commands see current values.\n\n## Trigger points (minimum)\n- `desloppify review --import ...`\n- `desloppify review --run-batches ...` (post-merge/import path)\n- `desloppify review --external-submit ...` (trusted external import path)\n- `desloppify resolve fixed ...`\n- `desloppify resolve wontfix ...`\n- `desloppify resolve false_positive ...` / reopen transitions\n- Any batch/command that applies auto-fixes and resolves findings as fixed\n\n## Output expectations\nFor score-moving commands, include a short “score update” block at end:\n- Overall (lenient + strict)\n- Objective + Subjective totals\n- Dimension deltas where changed (compact)\n- Wontfix debt signal (strict gap)\n\nExample shape:\n- `Strict: 82.4 -> 84.1 (+1.7)`\n- `Lenient: 90.2 -> 90.4 (+0.2)`\n- `Subjective: 71.0 -> 74.8 (+3.8)`\n- `Changed dimensions: naming_quality +6.0, error_consistency -1.0`\n\n## Implementation approaches\n### A) Centralized post-mutation hook (recommended)\nAdd one shared `post_score_mutation_update(...)` helper invoked by all score-changing commands.\n- Pros: consistent behavior, easier to maintain, no drift between commands.\n- Cons: requires command touchpoints audit.\n\n### B) Event-driven state mutation bus\nEmit “score-affecting event” from each command and have a single score updater subscriber.\n- Pros: scalable, cleaner architecture for future telemetry.\n- Cons: larger refactor.\n\n### C) Command-local recalculation only\nEach command recalculates and prints scores itself.\n- Pros: quick patch.\n- Cons: drift risk, duplicate logic, inconsistent UX.\n\n## Suggested rollout\n1. Implement centralized helper + wire into review import and resolve flows first.\n2. Add score-delta block + scorecard refresh output.\n3. Expand to all fix/apply flows that mutate finding status.\n4. Add guard tests for every trigger point.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- Every listed trigger point emits updated score output in the same command run.\n- `query.json` and persisted score state are updated immediately after each trigger.\n- No stale score reported after successful imports/resolutions.\n- Regression tests cover all trigger paths and ensure score delta block appears.\n- Performance remains acceptable (incremental update path preferred, full recompute fallback).\n\n## Nice-to-have\n- Add `--no-score-refresh` escape hatch for power users/scripts.\n- Add structured JSON score delta payload for machine consumers.\nEOF"
}
```
